=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given three integer sequences of length N, namely A=(A_1,A_2,\ldots,A_N), B=(B_1,B_2,\ldots,B_N), and C=(C_1,C_2,\ldots,C_N), and an integer K.
For each of the N^3 choices of integers i,j,k (1\leq i,j,k\leq N), compute the value A_iB_j + B_jC_k + C_kA_i. Among all these values, find the K-th largest value.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N K
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N
B_1 B_2 \ldots B_N
C_1 C_2 \ldots C_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1\leq N \leq 2\times 10^5
- 1\leq K \leq \min(N^3,5\times 10^5)
- 1\leq A_i,B_i,C_i \leq 10^9
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

2 5
1 2
3 4
5 6

Sample Output 1

31

The N^3=8 values are computed as follows:

- For (i,j,k)=(1,1,1): A_1B_1+B_1C_1+C_1A_1=1\times 3+3\times 5+5\times 1=23
- For (i,j,k)=(1,1,2): A_1B_1+B_1C_2+C_2A_1=1\times 3+3\times 6+6\times 1=27
- For (i,j,k)=(1,2,1): A_1B_2+B_2C_1+C_1A_1=1\times 4+4\times 5+5\times 1=29
- For (i,j,k)=(1,2,2): A_1B_2+B_2C_2+C_2A_1=1\times 4+4\times 6+6\times 1=34
- For (i,j,k)=(2,1,1): A_2B_1+B_1C_1+C_1A_2=2\times 3+3\times 5+5\times 2=31
- For (i,j,k)=(2,1,2): A_2B_1+B_1C_2+C_2A_2=2\times 3+3\times 6+6\times 2=36
- For (i,j,k)=(2,2,1): A_2B_2+B_2C_1+C_1A_2=2\times 4+4\times 5+5\times 2=38
- For (i,j,k)=(2,2,2): A_2B_2+B_2C_2+C_2A_2=2\times 4+4\times 6+6\times 2=44

Sorting these values in descending order, we have (44,38,36,34,31,29,27,23), so the 5th largest value is 31.

Sample Input 2

3 10
100 100 100
100 100 100
100 100 100

Sample Output 2

30000

Sample Input 3

5 54
800516877 573289179 26509423 168629803 696409999
656737335 915059758 201458890 931198638 185928366
140174496 254538849 830992027 305186313 322164559

Sample Output 3

689589940713840351

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Let's rigorously verify parent uniqueness and children set.

Define order of coordinates: i (A), j (B), k (C). Parent P(i,j,k):
- if i > 0: P=(i-1,j,k)
- else if j > 0: P=(0,j-1,k)
- else: P=(0,0,k-1) (k>0)
This chooses the first nonzero coordinate in order (i,j,k) and decrements it. Thus any triple except root has unique parent. The path from root to node increments coordinates in reverse order? Let's see: To reach node, parent chain decrements first nonzero coordinate. Thus to generate node, from parent increment the coordinate that was decremented. The parent is obtained by decrementing the first nonzero coordinate. Therefore child relation from node v to w if w's parent is v. That means w is obtained by incrementing some coordinate t, and all coordinates before t in w are zero? Let's derive.

Given v=(i,j,k). Which w=(i',j',k') have P(w)=v?
P(w) decrements first nonzero coordinate d of w:
- If i'>0, d=i, P=(i'-1,j',k') = v. So i'=i+1, j'=j, k'=k. This works for any v, and requires i'>0 automatically. So child w=(i+1,j,k) always.
- If i'=0 and j'>0, d=j, P=(0,j'-1,k')=v. Then v must have i=0, j=j'-1, k=k'. So if v.i=0, child w=(0,j+1,k). This requires j+1>0 yes.
- If i'=0 and j'=0, d=k, P=(0,0,k'-1)=v. Then v must have i=0,j=0, k=k'-1. So if v.i=0 and v.j=0, child w=(0,0,k+1).

Thus children set as above. Good.

Need ensure every node reachable from root via these children. For node (i,j,k):
- If i>0, parent (i-1,j,k), so recursively reach (0,j,k) by decrementing i? Actually parent chain decrements i until 0, so reach (0,j,k).
- From (0,j,k) if j>0, parent (0,j-1,k), so reach (0,0,k).
- From (0,0,k), parent (0,0,k-1), reach root.
Reverse path: root -> (0,0,k) by increment k (allowed at root and (0,0,k-1) because i=0,j=0) -> (0,j,k) by increment j at (0,j-1,k) because i=0 -> (i,j,k) by increment i at (i-1,j,k) always. Yes.

Thus no visited set needed. Great.

Now heap initialization: root (0,0,0) with value A[0]*B[0]+... sorted descending. Pop K times. Each pop, push children:
1. if i+1 < N: push (i+1,j,k)
2. if i == 0 and j+1 < N: push (0,j+1,k)
3. if i == 0 and j == 0 and k+1 < N: push (0,0,k+1)
Need be careful: For node with i=0,j=0, children include (1,0,k), (0,1,k), (0,0,k+1). Our rules:
- always i+1
- if i==0, j+1
- if i==0 and j==0, k+1
So yes.

But wait, from node (0,0,k), child (0,1,k) is generated by rule 2; child (0,0,k+1) by rule 3; child (1,0,k) by rule 1. Good.
From node (0,j,k) with j>0: children (1,j,k) and (0,j+1,k). Good.
From node (i>0,j,k): child (i+1,j,k) only. Does this mean from (1,0,0), cannot generate (1,1,0) directly, but (1,1,0) generated from (0,1,0) via increment i. Is (0,1,0) generated? From root: child (0,1,0) yes. Good.

Need verify enumeration order with equal values and child value <= parent. Since all values positive and sorted descending, increment coordinate decreases or equal. So heap property for no visited: At any time, all ungenerated nodes have parent generated? With unique parent, generated set is ancestor-closed (if node generated, parent generated because node is pushed from parent). Thus any ungenerated node's parent might be ungenerated; but consider the first node on path from root to it that is ungenerated; its parent is generated, so it is in heap. Thus heap max among ungenerated is in heap. Therefore popping K yields top K. Good.

Need consider duplicate values and stable order irrelevant.

So p in heap. Thus max not-yet-generated <= max heap. Heap nodes are unpopped, so max ungenerated >= max heap. Thus okay.

Now, let's think about computing value from code. We decode code each pop:

So:

This is two divmods. Good.

Then compute val = -neg (or we can compute from decoded indices? -neg is exact; no need compute). For children, compute new value from scratch. But we already have parent val; could compute child val as parent val + delta to avoid multiplications? Let's see:
- increment i: child_i=i+1, same j,k. delta_i = (A[i+1]-A[i]) * (B[j] + C[k]). Need B[j]+C[k].
- increment j (only i=0): child_j=j+1, same i=0,k. delta_j = (B[j+1]-B[j]) * (A[0] + C[k]).
- increment k (only i=0,j=0): child_k=k+1. delta_k = (C[k+1]-C[k]) * (A[0] + B[0]).
Since arrays sorted descending, deltas <=0. This uses one multiplication per child instead of 3. Also avoids indexing A[ci], etc. But need be careful with parent val. Could compute child_val = val + delta. This is faster and less memory? no. Let's verify formula:
f = A_i B_j + B_j C_k + C_k A_i.
If A changes from a to a':
f' = a'B + B C + C a' = BC + a'(B+C).
f = BC + a(B+C).
delta = (a'-a)(B+C). Yes.
If B changes from b to b':
f = A b + b C + C A = AC + b(A+C).
delta = (b'-b)(A+C). Yes.
If C changes from c to c':
f = A B + B c + c A = AB + c(A+B).
delta = (c'-c)(A+B). Yes.
So child_val = val + (new-old)*(sum of other two). This is correct.

But note for increment j when i=0, A[0] is used. For increment k when i=0,j=0, A[0]+B[0]. Good.

This delta method requires parent val and decoded indices. It may be faster. Need ensure if arrays have equal values, delta 0. Good.

Let's implement push_child as inline to avoid function call overhead? We can define a nested function? Function call for each child up to 1.5M might add overhead. Better inline in loop with repeated code. But code readability? We can write small local variables and repeated if blocks. We'll need push up to 3 children. Could define a helper `push(ci,cj,ck,cval)` but function call overhead maybe significant. Inline is better.

Pseudo:

import sys, heapq
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N,K = map(int,input().split())
    A = sorted(map(int,input().split()), reverse=True)
    B = sorted(map(int,input().split()), reverse=True)
    C = sorted(map(int,input().split()), reverse=True)
    heap = []
    val0 = A[0]*B[0] + B[0]*C[0] + C[0]*A[0]
    heapq.heappush(heap, (-val0, 0))
    NN = N*N
    for cnt in range(K):
        neg, code = heapq.heappop(heap)
        val = -neg
        if cnt == K-1:
            print(val)
            return
        tmp, k = divmod(code, N)
        i, j = divmod(tmp, N)
        # child i+1
        if i + 1 < N:
            ci = i+1
            nval = val + (A[ci] - A[i]) * (B[j] + C[k])
            heapq.heappush(heap, (-nval, code + NN))
        # child j+1 if i==0
        if i == 0 and j + 1 < N:
            cj = j+1
            nval = val + (B[cj] - B[j]) * (A[0] + C[k])
            heapq.heappush(heap, (-nval, code + N))
        # child k+1 if i==0 and j==0
        if i == 0 and j == 0 and k + 1 < N:
            ck = k+1
            nval = val + (C[ck] - C[k]) * (A[0] + B[0])
            heapq.heappush(heap, (-nval, code + 1))
    # Should not reach because K>=1 and loop returns at K-1
if __name__ == "__main__": main()

Need check code offsets:
code = i*N*N + j*N + k.
Increment i: code + N*N. Yes.
Increment j: code + N. Yes.
Increment k: code + 1. Yes.

But wait, if i==0 and j+1<N, child code = code + N. Suppose code = (0*NN + j*N + k). Add N => (j+1)*N + k. Good.
If i==0 and j==0 and k+1<N, child code = code +1. Good.
If i+1<N, child code = (i+1)*NN + j*N + k = code + NN. Good.

Need ensure child code corresponds to child indices and no overflow; Python fine.

Let's write code:

def main():
    import sys, heapq
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, K = map(int, input().split())
    A = sorted(map(int, input().split()), reverse=True)
    B = sorted(map(int, input().split()), reverse=True)
    C = sorted(map(int, input().split()), reverse=True)

    NN = N * N
    A0 = A[0]; B0 = B[0]; C0 = C[0]? Need C0? Not used except maybe no. A0+B0 used. A0 used. C0 not.
    root_val = A0 * B0 + B0 * C0 + C0 * A0
    heap = [(-root_val, 0)]
    heappush = heapq.heappush
    heappop = heapq.heappop
    # heapq.heapify not needed for single element
    for cnt in range(K):
        neg, code = heappop(heap)
        val = -neg
        if cnt == K - 1:
            print(val)
            return
        tmp, k = divmod(code, N)
        i, j = divmod(tmp, N)
        bj = B[j]
        ck = C[k]
        if i + 1 < N:
            nval = val + (A[i+1] - A[i]) * (bj + ck)
            heappush(heap, (-nval, code + NN))
        if i == 0 and j + 1 < N:
            nval = val + (B[j+1] - bj) * (A0 + ck)
            heappush(heap, (-nval, code + N))
        if i == 0 and j == 0 and k + 1 < N:
            nval = val + (C[k+1] - ck) * (A0 + B0)
            heappush(heap, (-nval, code + 1))

Need consider if `cnt == K-1` before pushing children; if K=1, no children. Good.

For earlier pops, pushes children. At the penultimate pop, it pushes the last node. At last pop, heap contains last node, pop and return. Good. If K=N^3=8 sample, at cnt=7 pop code7 and return. Heap before pop had code7 only? Let's simulate: after pop code5 (cnt4? Actually cnt indices) etc. At cnt=6 pop code6, heap had code7. At cnt=7 pop code7 return. Good.

If key = (-val) * BASE + code, when popping, to recover val and code:
code = key % BASE (but key is negative? In Python, % returns nonnegative if BASE positive. key = -val*BASE + code, with code < BASE. key % BASE = code. Good.)
val = - (key // BASE)? Need be careful with floor division for negative. key = -v*B + c. key // B = -v + floor(c/B) = -v because c<B. So val = - (key // B). Example v=100,c=5,B=1000: key=-100000+5=-99995. key//1000 = -100 (floor -99.995 = -100). val=100. key%1000=5. Good.
So one int stores both. Heap min by key: Since -v*B dominates code (|code|<B), comparing keys equivalent to comparing (-v, code). Because if v1>v2, -v1*B + c1 < -v2*B + c2? This is a great memory optimization. Need ensure BASE = N*N*N, which can be up to 8e15, fits in Python int. key magnitude up to 3e18 * 8e15 = 2.4e34, Python int ~ 116 bits, size 40 bytes? Let's write code with single-int heap:

import sys, heapq

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, K = map(int, input().split())
    A = list(map(int, input().split())); A.sort(reverse=True)
    B = list(map(int, input().split())); B.sort(reverse=True)
    C = list(map(int, input().split())); C.sort(reverse=True)

    BASE = N * N * N
    OFFSET = 3 * 10**18

    A0 = A[0]; B0 = B[0]
    root_val = A0 * B0 + B0 * C[0] + C[0] * A0
    heap = [(OFFSET - root_val) * BASE]  # code 0
    heappush = heapq.heappush
    heappop = heapq.heappop

    for cnt in range(K):
        key = heappop(heap)
        code = key % BASE
        t = key // BASE
        val = OFFSET - t
        if cnt == K - 1:
            print(val)
            return
        tmp, k = divmod(code, N)
        i, j = divmod(tmp, N)
        bj = B[j]
        ck = C[k]
        if i + 1 < N:
            nval = val + (A[i+1] - A[i]) * (bj + ck)
            heappush(heap, (OFFSET - nval) * BASE + (code + NN))  # need NN
        if i == 0 and j + 1 < N:
            nval = val + (B[j+1] - bj) * (A0 + ck)
            heappush(heap, (OFFSET - nval) * BASE + (code + N))
        if i == 0 and j == 0 and k + 1 < N:
            nval = val + (C[k+1] - ck) * (A0 + B0)
            heappush(heap, (OFFSET - nval) * BASE + (code + 1))

Need define NN = N*N. In code above, use `NN`. Good.

Wait, `BASE = N * N * N`, `NN = N * N`. For increment i, code + NN. For increment j, code + N. For increment k, code +1. Good.

But let's not finalize yet; let's consider if there is any hidden flaw in the unique parent enumeration for arbitrary monotone functions with equal values. We already reasoned. Let's maybe prove more formally in mind:
Let S be set of triples. Define parent p(t) as above. The directed graph from root to all nodes via children is a tree (each node has one parent). For each edge parent->child, child coordinate is parent coordinate +1 in one dimension, so value(child) <= value(parent). We maintain a max-heap of frontier (generated but not output). Initially root. When we output a node, add its children. This is exactly best-first search on a tree with non-increasing edge weights. It enumerates nodes in non-increasing value order. Is that always true for a tree with non-increasing edge weights? Yes, because for any unoutput node u, consider path from root to u. Let v be the first unoutput node on that path. Its parent is output, so v was added when parent was output. Thus v is in heap. Since values decrease along path from v to u, value(v) >= value(u). Therefore the maximum value among all unoutput nodes is at least value(v) and is in heap? Actually v is in heap, so heap max >= value(v) >= value(u). Thus heap max >= max unoutput. Conversely heap nodes are unoutput, so heap max <= max unoutput. Therefore equal. So output order is non-increasing and each pop is max unoutput. Good. This proof doesn't require edge weights strictly decreasing, only non-increasing. Good.

Now, let's think about if the tree includes all N^3 nodes. We argued yes. Let's prove by induction on code? Thus recursively reaches root. Conversely, children rules generate exactly nodes whose parent is current. So tree covers all nodes. Good.

Let's draft code:

import sys
import heapq

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, K = map(int, input().split())
    A = list(map(int, input().split()))
    B = list(map(int, input().split()))
    C = list(map(int, input().split()))
    A.sort(reverse=True)
    B.sort(reverse=True)
    C.sort(reverse=True)

    SHIFT = 54
    MASK = (1 << SHIFT) - 1
    CMASK = (1 << 18) - 1
    INC_I = 1 << 36
    INC_J = 1 << 18
    OFFSET = 3 * 10**18

    A0 = A[0]
    B0 = B[0]
    C0 = C[0]
    root_val = A0 * B0 + B0 * C0 + C0 * A0

    heap = [((OFFSET - root_val) << SHIFT)]
    heappush = heapq.heappush
    heappop = heapq.heappop
    A0B0 = A0 + B0

    for cnt in range(K):
        key = heappop(heap)
        code = key & MASK
        val = OFFSET - (key >> SHIFT)

        if cnt == K - 1:
            print(val)
            return

        k = code & CMASK
        j = (code >> 18) & CMASK
        i = code >> 36

        bj = B[j]
        ck = C[k]

        if i + 1 < N:
            nval = val - (A[i] - A[i+1]) * (bj + ck)
            heappush(heap, ((OFFSET - nval) << SHIFT) | (code + INC_I))

        if i == 0 and j + 1 < N:
            nval = val - (B[j] - B[j+1]) * (A0 + ck)
            heappush(heap, ((OFFSET - nval) << SHIFT) | (code + INC_J))

        if i == 0 and j == 0 and k + 1 < N:
            nval = val - (C[k] - C[k+1]) * A0B0
            heappush(heap, ((OFFSET - nval) << SHIFT) | (code + 1))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's consider if there is an alternative interpretation: "For each of the N^3 choices of integers i,j,k" means i,j,k each from 1..N, not necessarily sorted. We sort values, but each original index maps to a sorted position. The number of triples is N^3. Our enumeration over sorted positions counts each combination of values, but if there are duplicate values, the mapping from original indices to sorted positions is a bijection? Sorting a list of values with duplicates: each original element has a position in the sorted order (stable sort assigns distinct positions). Thus the set of triples of sorted positions corresponds bijectively to original triples. The f value for a triple of sorted positions equals f for the corresponding original values. So multiset of values identical. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible flaw in the best-first search on a tree with non-increasing edge weights: It outputs nodes in non-increasing value order, but does it output exactly top K if there are nodes with value greater than some heap node but not yet generated? We proved no. Let's re-evaluate with tree and non-increasing edge weights. At any point, heap contains generated but unpopped nodes. For any ungenerated node u (not in heap), consider path from root to u. Let v be the first node on this path that is not in heap? Wait "generated" means in heap or popped. If u not generated, there is a first node on path from root to u that is not generated. Its parent is generated. If parent is popped, then this node would have been generated (pushed) when parent was popped. Therefore parent cannot be popped; it must be in heap. Thus v's parent is in heap. Let p = parent. Since edge p->v has value(v) <= value(p). Also u is descendant of v, and values non-increasing along path, so value(u) <= value(v) <= value(p). Thus p in heap has value >= u. Therefore for every ungenerated node u, there is some heap node with value >= u. Hence max heap >= max ungenerated. Heap nodes are ungenerated (not popped), so max ungenerated >= max heap. Thus max heap = max ungenerated. So popping heap gives max ungenerated. This holds regardless of whether v is generated? We considered u not generated. If u is in heap, it's ungenerated too but included in max ungenerated. The equality max heap = max ungenerated holds because heap subset of ungenerated, and for any ungenerated u not in heap, there is heap node >= u. Thus max ungenerated <= max heap; and >= because heap subset. Good. So correct.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code if N=200000 and K=500000, heap operations with int keys maybe okay. Let's estimate heap size and operations more accurately:
Each pop pushes at most 3. But with unique parent, number of children:
- If i>0: 1 child (i+1) if i+1<N.
- If i=0,j>0: 2 children (i+1, j+1) if both in range.
- If i=0,j=0: 3 children (i+1, j+1, k+1) if in range.
So total pushes over K pops can be up to 3K but likely less. For K=5e5, heap size maybe up to 1e6. 1e6 ints in heap. Heap push/pop each O(log heap size) ~20. 2M operations *20 =40M sift steps. Each sift step compares ints (C) and swaps pointers. 40M maybe ~1-2 seconds in PyPy? Actually heapq is Python code, each sift loop is Python, 40M iterations could be ~3-5 seconds. But K=5e5, pushes <=1.5M, pops=0.5M, total heap operations=2M, each operation does log ~20 comparisons, so 40M Python loop iterations. This might be a bit high but acceptable in PyPy? AtCoder time limit unknown. Could optimize by reducing heap operations? Maybe use a different algorithm? Let's think if there is a faster approach.

Alternative: Use a heap over "frontier" with at most 3 children; 2M heap operations is typical for K=5e5. Python can handle ~2M heapq operations? Actually each heappush/pop is implemented in Python and does a while loop. 2M * log ~40M iterations; 40M Python loop iterations might be ~2-4 seconds in PyPy, maybe 6 in CPython. AtCoder time limit maybe 2 sec? Could be tight. Need consider if there is a more efficient algorithm.

Maybe there is an O(K log K) but with fewer heap operations? We can use a heap and push only 2 children per pop using a different parent scheme? For 3D, maybe each node can have at most 2 children if we define parent as decreasing the largest index? Let's explore.

Standard no-duplicate enumeration for Cartesian product can use "parent = decrease the last nonzero coordinate" or "increase the first coordinate" leading to at most 2 children? We used order i,j,k and got 3 for root, 2 for i=0, 1 for i>0. Average maybe less than 2? Let's analyze. In worst case, many popped nodes are i=0,j=0 (3 children) or i=0,j>0 (2 children). But number of i=0 nodes is N^2, which can be huge (4e10) but K=5e5, so could pop many i=0 nodes. If values arranged so all i=0 nodes have high values, then first 5e5 pops could be i=0 nodes, each pushing 2 or 3 children. That would be ~1.5M pushes. So heap operations ~2M. Can't avoid if using heap.

Could use a different parent scheme that gives each node at most 2 children? For d dimensions, a tree with unique parent can have at most d children (root). But maybe choose parent as decrementing the largest coordinate? Let's derive children count. If parent = decrement the largest nonzero coordinate? For node (i,j,k), parent:
- if k>0: (i,j,k-1)
- elif j>0: (i,j-1,0)
- else: (i-1,0,0)
Then children:
- increment k always? Parent of (i,j,k+1) if k+1>0 is (i,j,k), so yes.
- If k=0 and j>0? increment j? Parent of (i,j+1,0) if j+1>0 and k=0 is (i,j,0), so yes if k=0.
- If k=0,j=0? increment i? Parent of (i+1,0,0) if k=0,j=0 is (i,0,0), so yes if k=0,j=0.
This is symmetric to ours with order k,j,i. Children count: root 3, k=0,j=0 nodes 3, k=0,j>0 nodes 2, k>0 nodes 1. Same average.

Can we have a parent scheme with at most 2 children for all nodes? For 3 dimensions, maybe use "parent = decrease coordinate with largest index" yields children: increment each coordinate that is >= all others? Let's see. If parent is obtained by decreasing the largest coordinate (with tie order), then a child is obtained by increasing a coordinate that is currently the unique maximum? For node (i,j,k), children are those w such that parent(w)=node. Suppose parent(w) decreases the largest coordinate of w. For w to have parent node, w must be node with one coordinate incremented, and that incremented coordinate must be the largest in w (or tie-breaking). This may yield at most 2? Let's test. Define parent(w): let m = max(i,j,k), decrement the first coordinate attaining max? Then child of v=(i,j,k): w = v + e_t. Need t be the max coordinate in w (after increment) and tie-breaking choose it. For any v, how many t satisfy? Potentially 1 or 2? Let's examine. If v=(0,0,0), w=(1,0,0): max is i, parent decrements i -> (0,0,0), yes. w=(0,1,0): max j, yes. w=(0,0,1): max k, yes. So root 3. For v=(1,0,0), w=(2,0,0): max i, yes. w=(1,1,0): max i? i=1,j=1 tie; if tie decrement first max (i), parent would (0,1,0), not v. If tie decrement last max, parent (1,0,0), yes. So with tie-breaking, maybe more. This scheme might have more children. Not necessarily better.

Could use "SMAWK" or "selection in sorted matrices" to find K-th largest in O(N log N + K) maybe? But K=5e5, heap is okay.

Now, let's think about optimizing heap operations further:

- Could use `for cnt in range(K - 1):` pop and push, then pop answer? That would avoid checking cnt each iteration? Current check inside loop. We can do:
for _ in range(K - 1):
    key = heappop(heap)
    ... push children
key = heappop(heap)
print(OFFSET - (key >> SHIFT))
This avoids `if cnt == K-1` branch on every iteration. It also pushes children for first K-1 pops, not for Kth. This is slightly faster. Let's implement this way.

Pseudo:
for _ in range(K - 1):
    key = heappop(heap)
    code = key & MASK
    val = OFFSET - (key >> SHIFT)
    decode...
    push children
# K-th
key = heappop(heap)
print(OFFSET - (key >> SHIFT))
This is cleaner and faster. Need ensure K>=1. If K=1, range(0) no loop, pop root print. Good.
This also means for K=1, we don't decode/push. Good.

Let's adopt this loop structure.

Code:

for _ in range(K - 1):
    key = heappop(heap)
    code = key & MASK
    val = OFFSET - (key >> SHIFT)

    k = code & CMASK
    j = (code >> 18) & CMASK
    i = code >> 36

    bj = B[j]
    ck = C[k]

    if i + 1 < N:
        nval = val - (A[i] - A[i+1]) * (bj + ck)
        heappush(heap, ((OFFSET - nval) << SHIFT) | (code + INC_I))
    if i == 0 and j + 1 < N:
        nval = val - (B[j] - B[j+1]) * (A0 + ck)
        heappush(heap, ((OFFSET - nval) << SHIFT) | (code + INC_J))
    if i == 0 and j == 0 and k + 1 < N:
        nval = val - (C[k] - C[k+1]) * A0B0
        heappush(heap, ((OFFSET - nval) << SHIFT) | (code + 1))

key = heappop(heap)
print(OFFSET - (key >> SHIFT))

Need ensure if K-1 > number of nodes? K<=N^3, so after K-1 pops, heap nonempty. Good.

This avoids branch and pushes children for exactly K-1 pops. Good.

With fixed shifts, code order is lexicographic by (i,j,k). Let's see if all codes generated? For N=3, code = i<<36 | j<<18 | k. The tree generates nodes in code order? We saw with N=3 all equal, heap tie by code, it popped code0,1,2,3,...? Wait with fixed shifts, code for (0,0,1)=1, (0,0,2)=2, (0,1,0)=1<<18 (262144), not 3. The code order is not contiguous; it will pop all k for i=0,j=0 (codes 0,1,2), then j=1,k=0 (code 262144), then j=1,k=1 (262145), etc. But is (0,1,0) code 262144 generated before (0,0,2) code2? Root pushes (0,1,0) code262144 and (0,0,1) code1. Heap tie by code: code1 pops, pushes (0,1,1) code262145 and (0,0,2) code2 and (1,0,1) code big. Heap has code2, code262144, code262145. Pop code2, push (0,1,2) code262146, (1,0,2). Heap has code262144,262145,262146. Pop code262144 (0,1,0), push (1,1,0) code big, (0,2,0) code 2<<18. Heap has 262145,262146, 2<<18, etc. So code order is not fully contiguous but still all nodes generated. For N=3, all 27 nodes generated. Good.

- If i=0,j>0, parent (0,j-1,k). Parent rule: i==0 and (j-1)+1 < N, push j child. Yes.
- If i=0,j=0,k>0, parent (0,0,k-1). Parent rule: i==0,j==0 and (k-1)+1 < N, push k child. Yes.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
import heapq

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline

    N, K = map(int, input().split())
    A = list(map(int, input().split()))
    B = list(map(int, input().split()))
    C = list(map(int, input().split()))

    A.sort(reverse=True)
    B.sort(reverse=True)
    C.sort(reverse=True)

    SHIFT = 54
    MASK = (1 << SHIFT) - 1
    CMASK = (1 << 18) - 1
    INC_I = 1 << 36
    INC_J = 1 << 18

    OFFSET = 3 * 10**18

    A0 = A[0]
    B0 = B[0]
    C0 = C[0]
    A0B0 = A0 + B0

    root_val = A0 * B0 + B0 * C0 + C0 * A0
    heap = [((OFFSET - root_val) << SHIFT)]

    heappush = heapq.heappush
    heappop = heapq.heappop

    for _ in range(K - 1):
        key = heappop(heap)
        code = key & MASK
        val = OFFSET - (key >> SHIFT)

        k = code & CMASK
        j = (code >> 18) & CMASK
        i = code >> 36

        bj = B[j]
        ck = C[k]

        if i + 1 < N:
            nval = val - (A[i] - A[i + 1]) * (bj + ck)
            heappush(heap, ((OFFSET - nval) << SHIFT) | (code + INC_I))

        if i == 0 and j + 1 < N:
            nval = val - (B[j] - B[j + 1]) * (A0 + ck)
            heappush(heap, ((OFFSET - nval) << SHIFT) | (code + INC_J))

        if i == 0 and j == 0 and k + 1 < N:
            nval = val - (C[k] - C[k + 1]) * A0B0
            heappush(heap, ((OFFSET - nval) << SHIFT) | (code + 1))

    key = heappop(heap)
    print(OFFSET - (key >> SHIFT))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
